#          and 1 GiB over TP4, allocated during weight load BEFORE the KV
#          pool is sized. Fix the KEY, not the config rewrite: that rewrite
#          is load-bearing for non-YaRN derived lengths, and
#          utils/hf_transformers/common.py:682-698 (which zeroes the scaling
#          factor whenever original_max_position_embeddings is present) is
#          also why sglang's own derived context says 262,144 and why a
#          727,894-token prompt warning is a FALSE ALARM on this geometry.

#          THE 10-21 /root batch (entry 249, files outside git like
#          245/246): 9c.sh and 9.sh each take THREE functional lines at the
#          same relative position -- L3 cap 50G->150G, metadata TTL
#          300->30, and --random-seed 20260930 -- proven by functional-stream
#          md5 (9c d112bb0f..->c2dcb878.., 9.sh c069ec66..->5db87f98..) plus
#          a 539-field offline prepare_server_args diff of EXACTLY one field.
#          Say the seed's limits plainly: it does NOT make greedy traffic
#          reproducible (greedy bypasses the RNG entirely) and the A/B
#          harness benches at temperature 0.0, so a harness re-run cannot
#          demonstrate it either -- what it buys is temp>0 traffic plus the
#          boot-path RNG consumers, i.e. comparable boots. My own earlier
#          "~740 GB store, so a raise costs ~250 GB of blocking unlinks at
#          boot" was STALE and is corrected in the entry: the drain only
#          runs when _total_bytes > max_size, so a RAISE is free and only a
#          LOWERING pays. POWER, and a self-correction: an agent working in
#          this file asserted that power.max_limit is 325 W on ALL FOUR cards
#          and that both run scripts were therefore stale. IT WAS WRONG and
#          the scripts were right -- re-measured with
#          `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,power.limit,power.default_limit,power.min_limit,power.max_limit --format=csv`:
#          GPU0/GPU1 max_limit = 300 W (already AT their ceiling), GPU2/GPU3 =
#          325 W. So the headroom is 25 W on TWO of four cards, and because a
#          TP4 decode step is lockstep and bounded by the slowest rank, the two
#          UNRAISABLE cards stay the binding constraint -- raising GPU2/GPU3 buys
#          at most the straggler asymmetry and can even widen it. The box IS

  (tag: boot-fix) ===========================================================
  CUSTOM ALL-REDUCE v1 vs expandable_segments AT TP2 -- 2026-09-21 (LOCAL, no
  upstream provenance)
  ============================================================================
  # Symptom: moving the Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-FP8 serve from tp4 to tp2 on this
  # PCIe-only 4x RTX PRO 6000 box booted clean through weights, both memory
  # pools and the full target-verify graph, then died in CUDA graph capture:
  #   RuntimeError: invalid argument
  #   .../allreduce/custom_all_reduce.cuh:614      (cudaIpcGetMemHandle)
  #   Exception: Capture cuda graph failed: invalid argument
  # via decode_cuda_graph_runner.py -> parallel_state.graph_capture ->
  #   custom_all_reduce.py:241 register_graph_buffers
  #   -> custom_all_reduce_ops.get_graph_buffer_ipc_meta
  # The canned advice ("lower mem-fraction-static / cuda-graph-max-bs") is a
  # red herring -- 9.17 GB was free and all 23 verify graphs had captured.
  # Cause: the run sets PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True, so
  # torch's caching allocator hands out cuMemCreate/cuMemMap (VMM) pointers.
  # cudaIpcGetMemHandle accepts only cudaMalloc pointers and returns
  # cudaErrorInvalidValue on VMM ones. CustomAllreduce (v1) registers the
  # captured graph input buffers -- which are model activations from the torch
  # allocator -- through exactly that call. Its own workspace is fine: it uses
  # a raw lib.cudaMalloc in create_shared_buffer, so only the graph path is
  # affected, and only under CUDA graphs (i.e. always, in practice).
  # Why tp4 never hit it: v1 self-disables at world_size > 2 without NVLink
  # ("not supported on more than two PCIe-only GPUs"), and v2 -- which DOES
  # have a VMM path (_register_graph_inputs checks is_vmm_pointer and routes
  # to VmmGraphInputManager) -- is admitted only on full NVLink
  # (can_use_custom_all_reduce_v2 -> can_use_custom_all_reduce_with_nvlink).
  # So tp4 = both disabled -> NCCL -> fine. tp2 on PCIe = v2 rejected for no
  # NVLink, v1 accepted because world_size == 2, and v1 had no VMM guard.
  # That gap (v2 guards VMM, v1 does not) is the bug.
  # Fix: give v1 the guard v2 already has. New public
  # is_vmm_backed_allocator(device) in utils/cuda_vmm_utils.py (probe one byte,
  # is_vmm_pointer on it; False when cuda.bindings is absent so the historical
  # cudaMalloc assumption is preserved). custom_all_reduce.py calls it right
  # after the nvlink check and returns disabled with a warning naming
  # expandable_segments. v2's private _is_vmm_backed_allocator now delegates to
  # the shared helper so there is one implementation.
  # Effect: all-reduce falls back to NCCL, which is what tp4 already uses --
  # no regression, and the tp2 server boots. Pure Python, so it takes effect
  # without rebuilding sgl_kernel (the installed .so is built from
  # sglang.prev, hence the sglang.prev path in the traceback).
  # Regression guard (CPU, no GPU needed):
  # test/registered/unit/distributed/test_custom_all_reduce_vmm_guard.py
  # drives CustomAllreduce.__init__ with world_size 2 / no NVLink / VMM True
  # and asserts it stays disabled AND never reaches create_shared_buffer (a
  # half-built __init__ would leak IPC-shared buffers); the paired case pins
  # that a cudaMalloc allocator is unaffected.
         75. a89d298bac  fix(allreduce): disable legacy custom all-reduce on
             a VMM-backed allocator and share the VMM probe with v2
      # Verified: PYTHONPATH=python python -m pytest
      #   test/registered/unit/distributed/test_custom_all_reduce_vmm_guard.py
      #   -> 2 passed (and 1 failed, "assert False is True", with the guard
      #      block deleted -- the test does catch the bug).
      #   test/registered/unit/distributed/test_custom_all_reduce_v2_capability
      #   .py -> 4 passed (delegation change is behavior-preserving).
      #   Probe discriminates: is_vmm_backed_allocator(cuda:0) is False with
      #   the default allocator and True under expandable_segments:True.
      # Serving config that triggered it: /root/run_sglang_qwenflashnext8.sh
      #   (tp2/ep2 on GPUs 0-1, mem-fraction-static 0.90, weights 63.01 GB +
      #   MTP 2.68 GB, mamba pinned at 256 slots = 7.14 GB, KV 1587520 tokens,
      #   9.17 GB free at "Memory pool end"). Keep expandable_segments ON --
      #   it is load-bearing at that memory pressure.

  PR #40748  [open]  @huangtingwei9988
      https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/40748
      [HiCache] Avoid over-eviction by reclaiming only allocation shortfalls
         90. e3ccc45aff  fix(hicache): reclaim only allocation shortfalls on host and device
             <- 022dff56762b7b412ebfb33d4ac33393b6cdf8ab
      # When a host/device allocation failed, HiCache evicted the FULL request
      # size regardless of free capacity, discarding cached prefixes for
      # nothing. Now evicts only need - available_size(), and skips eviction
      # when the pool already has room. Our path: UnifiedRadixCache + MAMBA
      # (HostPoolGroup.alloc, MambaComponent.alloc_prefetch_staging, L3
      # prefetch host alloc, retraction backup); the SWA and legacy HiRadix
      # hunks are inert here. Author A/B (GLM-4.7-Flash, H20): eviction
      # 12,288 -> 4,096 tokens, next-request TTFT 1,145 -> 95 ms.
      # Safe by construction: every host pool's alloc(n) returns None iff
      # n > available_size(), so a failed alloc always has shortfall > 0.
      # Picked the PR's single real commit; its "Merge branch 'main'"
      # (e3ecb991fd) skipped. Clean cherry-pick -x, no conflicts. The PR
      # ships no tests (author removed them) and breaks one existing test
      # -> entry 91.
         91. 82cb963b10  [HiCache] Retarget the SWA staging-exhausted test to shortfall-sized reclaim
      # Test-only follow-up (same pattern as 81). test_prepare_prefetch_swa_
      # pool_exhausted mocked alloc()->None but left available_size() real,
      # so the pool looked free and evict_host was correctly skipped under 90.
      # Now also mocks available_size()=0; still pins "retry evicts SWA, not
      # FULL" and passes on pre-90 code too. Upstream CI will hit the same
      # failure on the PR as-is.
      # Verified (.venv2, live server holding all 4 GPUs): 122-file battery
      # (unit/mem_cache/* + hisparse_unit + stat_loggers_di) per-file
      # results IDENTICAL before/after. test_unified_radix_cache_unittest.py
      # under xdist -n 6 --max-worker-restart: base 1130P/128F -> after
      # 1135P/123F, after-failures a strict subset of base (all the
      # cudaMemcpyBatchAsync segfault, see CAVEATS). Scratch CPU check of the
      # real HostPoolGroup.alloc / MambaComponent.alloc_prefetch_staging
      # with fake pools: reclaims 7 of 10 / 7 of 12, 0 when already free.

  Mixed-chunk prefill enablement (09-28, branch mixed-chunk-fix):
      # --enable-mixed-chunk was accepted at startup (NEXTN -> EAGLE, which
      # supports_mixed_chunk() since #36933) but broke at runtime in four ways:
      #  1. CRASH: a mixed batch appends every running decode as a 1-token
      #     extend row with prefix seq_len-1; compressed QSA's extend write plan
      #     asserted prefix % 4 == 0 -> device-side assert on both TP ranks at
      #     the first mixed step, NCCL watchdog abort, server gone 12 s after
      #     boot (reproduced here, TP2 on GPUs 2-3).
      #  2. SILENT: merge_batch dropped the extend rows' mamba track tensors
      #     after prepare_for_extend stamped mamba_last_track_seqlen -> an
      #     unwritten ping-pong slot was donated to the radix tree (#39342).
      #  3. SILENT (fp32 SSM only): circular GDN ReplaySSM keeps `temporal` at
      #     cache_base; the plain extend kernel used for decode tails ignored the
      #     ring. BF16 folds on every commit, so our bf16 config never hit it.
      #  4. OOM: the PLE short conv padded rows x longest-row x channels; a 4k
      #     chunk + 40 decode tails asked for 2.47 GiB at 90% memory.

        125. 738eea94f0  QSA: let the pending index-K ring hold more than one compression group
             <- 31b7edb6216cf1bd980e9046e085789b0efec4d2   PR #40143 [open]
      # LOCALLY MODIFIED: fixes a PRE-EXISTING verify-window collision (not
      # mixed-chunk specific). The one-group ring (position % 4) let MTP
      # verify rows overwrite the slot of a member 4 positions earlier in the
      # same forward before the group was compressed: 3 of 4 window
      # alignments, 84/89 committed groups wrong in the new indexer test. The
      # PR sizes the ring ceil(draft/ratio) = 1 for us; here
      # ceil((draft+ratio-1)/ratio) = 2, guard draft <= ratio*(N-1)+1.
      # Only affects sparse block selection past the 2048-token budget.
      #   e2e recall over the model's own generated list (greedy, 48 req):
      #     ~5.7k tokens: 240/240 with 1 group and with 2 (not sensitive)
      #     ~23k tokens:  160/160 with 1 group and with 2 -- no measurable e2e
      #     effect on this model (the bad key blends in a neighbour 4 tokens
      #     later); kept as a correctness fix. Final build: GSM8K 0.967,
      #     mixed-prefix 0.965, soak clean.

        209. 8aab510bdc  hybrid L3 prefetch: end a capacity-shortened span on a VERIFIED trailing sidecar endpoint (+ 3fa5197482 sidecar reporting)
      # prod symptom, 59 events x 4 TP ranks: "Failed to fetch <64hex>.mamba
      # from HiCacheFile storage." + "HiCache hybrid prefetch discarded
      # req=... completed=2944 requested=2944 kv_beliefs_kept_pages=46
      # aux_pool_hit_pages={'mamba': 0}" -- 60 discards = 222,720 tokens read
      # from L3 and thrown away (unfulfilled{storage_transfer}), host pools
      # pinned at 3,082,496/3,082,560 tokens and 316/316 mamba states.
      # Root cause: batch_exists_v2 verifies the sidecar at TRAILING page B,
      # but the host-capacity clamp in allocate_storage_hit shortens the span
      # to an interior page (unified_radix_cache.py:3112 truncates
      # operation.hash_value to alloc_len) and _sync_trailing_keys then reads
      # <interior>.mamba -- sidecars cover only ~1 in 26 stored pages, so the
      # read always missed and _check_hybrid_prefetch_result's all-or-nothing
      # rule discarded the whole (fully transferred) KV prefix. Fix:
      # HiCacheFile.batch_exists_v2 reports the trailing pools' SHARED stop
      # points as restorable_prefix_pages (the Mooncake/linker contract this
      # backend was missing -- final_pages is now that shared point), the
      # agreed set rides the ONE existing MIN collective of
      # _sync_prefetch_hit_query as a 0/1 mask of length
      # len(token_ids)//page_size + 1 (rank-invariant shape even when a rank
      # skips or fails its query; MIN == AND, so every rank clamps to the same
      # endpoint), and allocate_storage_hit ends a shortened span on the
      # deepest agreed endpoint or declines it (host_capacity revoke -- queued
      # for retry, no doomed IO, no misleading discard log). Query-only: no
      # storage key or layout change, so the 285k pages + 14.9MB states
      # already in /root/hicache_store stay valid; a full-length span behaves
      # exactly as before, and buffer_only mode keeps its pinned endpoint
      # (sidecar_hash_values) and its allow_partial=False path untouched. New
      # CPU test test_hicache_trailing_endpoint.py (17 tests, 2 of them
      # 2-rank gloo): RED at base = 13 failures incl. the 24-token doomed
      # launch and a "no shared stop point" mask; stale expectation corrected
      # in test_hicache_fast_file_unit.py (two sidecars at pages 0/1, no
      # shared endpoint -> kv_hit_pages 2 -> 0, per-pool verdicts kept).
      # 3fa5197482 makes the silent paths talk: HiCacheFile.get names the pool
      # and the endpoint semantics on a miss, set() counts the LRU /
      # SGLANG_HICACHE_FILE_BACKEND_MIN_FREE_SPACE refusals per pool (mamba
      # write-amp was invisible: 22.4M tokens backed up vs 447k prefetched),
      # and the controller counts endpoint declines, verified trailing reads
      # and sidecar backup shortfalls -- 8 warnings each then quiet, counters
      # are class attributes for a later /metrics hook. P2 (clamp the usable
      # prefix at ack time instead of forfeiting the read) NOT shipped: needs
      # the endpoint published through PrefetchAck and the mamba seqlen stamp
      # re-derived at the clipped length, or a wrong-output risk (#39209
      # alignment) replaces a clean miss; the new decline counters size what
      # it would win. GPU debt: TP4 prefetch with a partial host clamp on the
      # live store + test/registered/hicache/* backend runs.

        210. 95142a935f  QSA chunked prefill: serve the paged kernel when the dense KV pack OOMs (+ 8bc0bfd91c pre-allocation byte budget, + c17a10d737 lint follow-up)
      # prod symptom, next9 2026-10-18 23:26 (26m47s after ready): all four TP
      # ranks died in one batch -- torch.OutOfMemoryError "Tried to allocate
      # 2.00 GiB" at qsa/sparse_attn.py:640 (pack_qsa_prefill_kv) via
      # qwen_sparse_attn_backend.forward_extend:2179 on the NEXTN
      # _draft_extend_for_prefill (eager; prefill graphs disabled) -- and
      # run_scheduler_process turned it into a SIGQUIT. Prefill OOM has NO
      # recovery path in sglang: retraction is decode-only and counts tokens,
      # never bytes (upstream #26266 closed unmerged, #30505 = this exact crash,
      # bot-closed unfixed).
      # Mechanism: the pack is sized by total_k = sum(sequence_lens) -- FULL
      # contexts (prefix + extend) summed over the batch -- at 1 KV head/rank x
      # 256 dim x bf16 = 512 B per summed token per tensor (~1 KiB/token for the
      # K+V pair), allocated FRESH per QSA layer per forward (12 target + 1
      # draft). No scheduler accounting bounds that sum:
      # --chunked-prefill-size / --max-prefill-tokens bill the EXTEND rows only,
      # and --enable-mixed-chunk folds every running decode request's whole
      # context into it. Shared radix prefixes let it exceed what is resident --
      # 2.00 GiB => total_k ~= 4.19M summed rows vs ~2.07M tokens in use (usage
      # 0.33 of the 6,246,400 pool) against 6.60 GiB of dynamic headroom at boot
      # -- and upstream #41933 (047405f534, via merge #4) DOUBLED this transient:
      # two fp8 index_select gathers -> one fused bf16 pack, a latency win with
      # no memory guard. Same call site killed prod 09-04 x2, 09-05, 09-09,
      # 09-11 (367k ctx); fork 8e831dc8a6 halved the peak (4x -> 2x) by joining
      # gather INDICES, its comment recording that it does not remove the
      # densification.
      # 95142a935f serves the forward instead of dying. The dense block (pack
      # AND the scaled-FP8 index_select) moved into _forward_chunk_prefill
      # inside except torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError, and
      # _chunk_prefill_oom_fallback runs the paged kernel -- hoisted into
      # _paged_chunk_prefill_output so the configured paged path and both
      # fallbacks cannot drift -- with ONE warning per process naming layer,
      # bytes, total_k, pool layout, free + allocator-cached headroom. The call
      # is STICKY: _chunk_prefill_kv_forced_paged is consulted FIRST by
      # _chunk_prefill_kv_path(), since a re-attempt would OOM once per QSA
      # layer per forward and the paged kernel allocates nothing. When
      # _paged_kv_source_supported() refuses -- the page-major KV envelope
      # (--enable-page-major-kv-layout, implied by --enable-unified-memory) or a
      # quant method serving get_key_buffer as a whole-layer dequantised
      # temporary (needs_plain_kv_dequant_read(); req_to_token slots do not
      # address it) -- or when the kill switch is off, it logs an error naming
      # the levers (--mem-fraction-static, --context-length,
      # --max-running-requests, --prefill-max-requests, plus KV_SOURCE=paged
      # only where usable) and RE-RAISES unchanged: no faked recovery. With no
      # OOM nothing changes, capture included: the only new device reads are
      # mem_get_info/allocator counters inside the diagnostic, and
      # _qsa_stream_is_capturing() (get_is_capture_mode() OR
      # torch.cuda.is_current_stream_capturing()) suppresses mem_get_info
      # because it synchronizes.
      # 8bc0bfd91c makes the catch rare -- never attempt a pack that cannot fit.
      # Sizing lives in ONE place, qsa_pack_prefill_kv_bytes(total_k, heads,
      # dim, dtype) = total_k*heads*dim*itemsize*2, next to the two torch.empty
      # calls it prices (the gate and the OOM message both read it, so the
      # estimate cannot drift from the reservation). _dense_pack_mem_route runs
      # before the dense materialisation and mirrors the proven #39095 idiom of
      # layers/attention/mqa_logits_utils.py: budget = min(total_memory *
      # TOTAL_FRACTION, (1 - mem_fraction_static) * total_memory *
      # FREE_FRACTION, (driver-free + allocator-cached) * FREE_FRACTION). The
      # first two terms are configuration only (device properties +
      # get_schedule().mem_fraction_static), hence capture-safe; the headroom
      # term is read at most ONCE per forward and cached in the chunk-prefill
      # hoist (QSA_PACK_HEADROOM_CACHE_KEY); an unknowable budget never refuses.
      # Below SGLANG_QSA_CHUNK_PREFILL_PACK_BUDGET_MIN_BYTES (512 MiB) the gate
      # returns before any arithmetic, so ordinary chunks pay no device query
      # and keep exactly the old fast path (asserted). Over budget, tiered, each
      # tier warn-once-per-process: (a) the paged kernel when the guard holds;
      # (b) else, on an FP8 pool, gather in the POOL dtype (half the bytes) and
      # dequantise inside the attention kernel -- equivalence read from the
      # kernel, not assumed: sparse_gqa_fwd_interface_triton_ck derives
      # kv_is_fp8 in _validate_sparse_gqa_dtypes (FP8 K/V against BF16/FP16 q is
      # accepted), passes _unit_scale(k_scale)/_unit_scale(v_scale), and the
      # kernel does keys.to(q_values.dtype) before tl.dot then scales by
      # k_scale/v_scale -- the same plain cast the pack itself performs (hence
      # use_pack's unit-descale rule) on the call shape the scaled-FP8 branch
      # always exercised, **scale_kwargs intact; (c) else attempt the pack
      # anyway and let the OOM fallback catch it -- a refusal with no
      # alternative is strictly worse than a wrong estimate. Counters:
      # _qsa_pack_attempts / _qsa_pack_budget_refusals / _qsa_pack_oom_fallbacks
      # (plain ints, no metrics plumbing).
      # Prod arithmetic: a 94.97 GiB card at --mem-fraction-static 0.90 gives a
      # ~1.9 GiB budget, which refuses the ~4 GiB K+V pair that killed next9,
      # while a 16k-token chunk (~16 MiB) stays far under the floor. The dump

        211. 8bcc61f491  Triton: device-load cached cubins during engine init (triton_load_watch reports -> prevents) + dummy-launch recipes for the known serving specializations
      # Why: Triton device-loads each specialization's cubin at its FIRST launch
      # (CompiledKernel._init_handles -> cuModuleLoadData), OUTSIDE the torch
      # caching allocator, so on an engine whose pools took all the memory a late
      # load is a scheduler-fatal OOM. Both next9 logs are evidence: the crashing
      # run has 46 "expandable_segments: memory mapping failed" lines and 24 late
      # device-loads (6 names x 4 ranks) at 0.15 GiB free while still serving;
      # the replacement run logs 92 late loads over 12 distinct names, all at
      # 0.69-0.73 GiB free, with ZERO slow-compile warnings -- i.e. every one was
      # a TRITON DISK-CACHE HIT whose CompiledKernel object is constructed by the
      # serving launch itself. That is the key fact: the enumeration pass (A)
      # alone would have caught none of them, so the dummy-launch recipes (B) are
      # the operative fix.
      # A (utils/triton_load_watch.py): install() now also chains
      # CompiledKernel.__init__ to record every kernel the process constructs
      # (weakrefs only -- the recorder never extends a kernel's life; a
      # pre-existing knobs.compilation.listener is chained, not replaced), and
      # prewarm_device_loads(worker) -- one delegated call line in
      # Scheduler.run_event_loop between install() and mark_serving_started(), so
      # after weights and graph capture and before any request -- calls
      # _init_handles() on each recorded kernel whose ``module`` is still None,
      # reusing Triton's own residency probe so kernels graph capture already
      # loaded are counted, not re-loaded. A second enumeration source is
      # triton.runtime.jit._triton_jit_function_registry device_caches, walked
      # through values() (never index a defaultdict key -- that inserts an empty
      # cache). Every load is individually guarded (a failure is a warning naming
      # the kernel, capped at 10, never a boot failure), the whole sweep is
      # guarded, the sweep stops with a warning naming the skipped count once free
      # memory drops below the floor (and then skips B too; B runs after A so the
      # dummy launches inherit any cubin A just resident-ed), and it always logs
      # one summary line: "Triton prewarm: loaded N cubins (M already resident, K
      # skipped, free device mem X.XX -> Y.YY GiB, Z.ZZ s)". Idempotent;
      # CUDA-only -- CPU CI, MLX, XPU, ROCm and a process where install() never
      # ran all no-op.
      # B (utils/triton_prewarm_ops.py, new): prewarm_known_kernels(device,
      # worker) dummy-launches the 8 _RECIPES that serving hits late, with the
      # constexprs read from LIVE geometry (page size, req_to_token width,
      # replayssm ring length, QSA compress ratio / compressed page / num_groups
      # / sharding world+rank, vocab size and logits dtype) and the {==1, %16,
      # other} int classes swept as BS_BUCKETS = (1, 2, 16): alloc_extend_kernel,
      # assign_req_to_token_pool, _fused_replay_state_indices_kernel,
      # _advance_gdn_spec_cursors_kernel,
      # _finish_gdn_replayssm_circular_fold_kernel, launch_graph_metadata (mode 0
      # decode / 1 target-verify / 2 draft-extend, which covers
      # _qsa_graph_row_metadata_kernel and _qsa_graph_layout_kernel; x
      # num_padding {0,1,bs} x seq_offset {0,1}), prepare+launch_draft_graph_-
      # metadata (_qsa_draft_graph_metadata_kernel -- the step count is baked in
      # via tl.static_range, so it is read live), and
      # apply_token_bitmask_inplace_triton (grammar bitmask, width = ceil(V/32)).
      # Every argument list is copied from a NAMED real call site or registered
      # test (the QSA recipes are the ones test_qsa.py's
      # test_qsa_graph_metadata_kernels_match_legacy_host_path and
      # test_qsa_draft_metadata_multi_step_graph validate): an invented value
      # warms a cubin serving never uses and wastes the very memory this is
      # about. Safety: all-zero inputs keep the page loops empty and the stores
      # masked; recipes allocate their own tiny buffers and never touch request
      # state or the KV/mamba pools -- the QSA metadata kernels read exactly ONE
      # int32 from the live req_to_token table (row 0, token 0, the last_loc each
      # launch loads) and store only here. Each recipe is guarded: a missing
      # attribute or a changed signature downgrades to a debug log. Coverage
      # against the observed late loads: 48 of the live run's 92, and 24/24 of
      # the crashing run's.
      # Deliberately NOT warmed (reasons in the module docstring, so nobody reads
      # _RECIPES as exhaustive): _small_copy_kernel (BIT_WIDTHS comes from the
      # live CudaGraphBufferRegistry slot groups), fused_moe_kernel (one
      # specialization per tuning bucket -- the Max-Q retune/precompile is a
      # separate owed lever), _qsa_mqa_prefill_kernel (live indexer head count /
      # head_dim), _layer_norm_fwd_1pass_kernel and _causal_conv1d_fwd_kernel
      # (model-forward config space), and the QSA graph-metadata kernels under
      # --qsa-cache-sharding-size > 1 (the live page table belongs to one shard,
      # so the recipe declines rather than guess).
      # Envs (environ.py, Torch Compile block):
      # SGLANG_ENABLE_TRITON_PREWARM_DEVICE_LOADS = EnvBool(True) -- 0 is the
      # rollback to lazy device-loads; SGLANG_TRITON_PREWARM_MIN_FREE_GB =
      # EnvFloat(1.0), deliberately matching the pre-existing
      # SGLANG_TRITON_LOAD_WARNING_THRESHOLD_GB -- every prod late load happened
      # below both.
      # Tests: NEW test/registered/unit/utils/test_triton_load_watch_prewarm.py
      # (register_cpu_ci est_time=10) 30P under pytest on BOTH triton builds --
      # .venv2 3.7.1 and the prod .venv 3.8.0: loads unloaded fake kernels,
      # skips resident ones, survives a raising _init_handles, stops at the floor
      # and skips the recipes, kill-switch / no-CUDA / ROCm / no-install no-ops,
      # idempotence, dead weakrefs pruned without the test owning a kernel, the
      # __init__ wrap chaining the original and not double-wrapping, the JIT scan

      # names (the request-driven warmup runs AFTER mark_serving_started, so its
      # own first loads still warn legitimately, as do the declined names);
      # recipe coverage via the debug log. Residual risk, stated on purpose:
      # recipes warm specialization CLASSES, not values -- a pointer-alignment or
      # slicing flip at a new call site can still miss, and that load falls back

      # sparse_gqa_packed_decode_with_lse_triton set kv_lens = topk (upstream
      # #43527), and our f9ddea14c0 kernel bound masks token >= kv_len -- but

      # slots in [0, pool) (torch._assert_async), and the max() keeps the

        217. faf53739e9 35b39aa72a  PRs #43108 + #44614  hybrid L3 rank beliefs and the prefetch-deadline anchor
      # #44614 is the HIGHEST-VALUE item of this batch: prod runs
      # --hicache-storage-prefetch-policy timeout. The timeout budget now
      # anchors on the FIRST BLOCKED ADMISSION POLL via the new
      # srt/utils/poll_timer.py PollTimer (PrefetchMetadata.timer)
      # instead of restarting from operation.start_time -- that start is
      # older than the wait and the hit chain has shrunk by then, so a
      # request could sit forever while the host tiers were pinned.
      # Three consequences fixed: a deadline cut used to be reported as
      # reason=storage_transfer, WHICH HEALED storage-existence beliefs
      # for pages the store still holds (poisoned positives -> later
      # prefetches skipped); and a rank that had not reached its poll
      # could re-issue a prefetch its peers had dropped. Now
      # operation.deadline_expired -> _finish_storage_prefetch(...,
      # reason="deadline") (unified_radix_cache.py:2447) and the
      # PrefillAdder closes admission on the retraction (:2489).
      # #43108: existence-belief invalidation moved OUT of
      # revoke_pending_prefetch into the rank-synchronized drain, and
      # SKIPPED when the reduced result carries no KV verdict at all
      # (`if PoolName.KV not in verdicts: return`) -- -1 means a peer
      # skipped its query, 0 means a peer missed; the same MIN==AND
      # reasoning as entry 209, applied one layer up.
      # THIS CORRECTS the entry-209 note "NOT fixed upstream": upstream
      # now attacks the BELIEF half; our trailing-ENDPOINT fix is still
      # the only repair for the capacity-clamped hybrid span (P2 stays
      # open).
      # Verified against our extended collective payload (1 + len(pools)
      # + restorable_mask): buffer_mode_sidecar 6P/6sub (+1 -- the PR's
      # duck-typed test passed UNMODIFIED against a SimpleNamespace
      # controller), storage_prefetch_lifecycle 15P/24sub (was
      # 10P/10sub), trailing_endpoint 17P, fast_file 54P/13sub, the
      # rest of the hicache set 0F. GPU debts: live TP4 partial-clamp
      # prefetch with deadline accounting, and the absence of belief
      # poisoning under it. from apply-p.

        243. no code  MEASURED VERDICT  FP8 vs NVFP4 on this box: +4.3% per-request, +28.5% KV, and why the win is capped
      # METHOD (read-only; artifacts /tmp/opencode/nvfp4-vs-fp8/): context+batch-
      # standardized medians over the common (bs x ctx) bucket cells, n>=20 both
      # sides. NVFP4 = sglang_next9c.log, ONE boot 04:25:36->13:34:24 (36,897
      # decode / 18,247 prefill lines) vs a SAVED COPY of the FP8 window
      # 03:23:34-04:25:08 (4,574 decode lines) + the controlled corpus
      # /root/ab_results/20261001T221030Z (24 arms; controls 00_control_base x5).
      # VERDICT: NVFP4 is genuinely FASTER, modestly. Implied decode step -3.7%
      # (n-weighted ratio 0.9634, per-cell 0.900-1.005); per-request generated
      # tok/s +4.3% (bs1 +4.3, bs2-3 +3.6, bs4-8 +2.2, best cell bs1/128-256k
      # +10.6); prefill equal-or-faster (8-16k cold +2.4, cache-hit +13.2).
      # ACCEPT NOT DEGRADED: pooled 2.729 (FP8) vs 2.695 (FP4) is a context-mix
      # Simpson artifact -- at matched bs1 context NVFP4 is HIGHER (2.744/2.613).
      # WHY CAPPED: the tensor scan shows ONLY the 48 target expert sets differ
      # (299,545 vs 152,089 records; mtp experts byte-identical FP8-block in both;
      # attention/GDN/router/shared-expert/hyper-connection/embed/lm_head BF16 in
      # both). Experts 49.5% -> 35.6% of per-GPU decode bytes => byte ceiling step
      # x0.783 at bs1 / x0.634 at bs8, but only ~10-20% of it is realized: implied
      # achieved BW ~348 GB/s of ~1.8 TB/s -- the step is NCCL/launch/occupancy-
      # bound, not weight-bound. Advantage GROWS with bs as predicted (38.8
      # distinct experts at bs1 -> 500 at bs48).
      # REFUTED, with line evidence. (a) --ep 1/--ep 2 does NOT unlock the SM120
      # small-row kernel: the gate reads hidden/intermediate from the PER-RANK SHARD
      # (modelopt_quant.py:3165-3166, intermediate//moe_tp_size per
      # fused_moe_triton/layer.py:420), so EP1/TP4 gives 160 and the
      # `intermediate_size % 64` clause (:2422) fails -> workspace None;
      # unreachable at every ep in {1,2,4} on TP4. (b) EP1 ALSO drops the FP8-block
      # MTP draft to BLOCK_SIZE_K=32 (fp8.py:1374-1416, gcd(128,160)=32). (c) EP1's
      # allreduce cost is a WASH -- fused_moe_triton/layer.py:1607 already issues
      # one TP all-reduce per MoE layer when ep>1, and per-rank weight bytes are
      # constant. (d) CUTLASS autotune DOES cover decode: this arm's own
      # /root/.cache/sglang/flashinfer/autotune/0.7.2/sm120/*/rank_tp0_pp0_dp0.json
      # has gemm1|gemm2 x 21 buckets M=1..8192, no tactic=-1. (e) the FP4 act-quant
      # tax is real (~48 extra quantize launches per forward; flashinfer refuses
      # fused GEMM1 quant unless MXFP8, fused_moe/runners.py:3841, block_m=16
      # padding) but is NOT a net penalty, and the triton FP8 arm pays its own.
      # (f) --mem-fraction-static has no per-step effect while
      # SGLANG_QSA_CHUNK_PREFILL_KV_SOURCE=paged (the pack budget it feeds
      # short-circuits, qwen_sparse_attn_backend.py:2755-2759); occupancy peaked at
      # 27% with zero evictions/retracts and queue 0 on every decode line in both
      # arms, so capacity levers are dead weight right now.
      # THE GATE ITSELF (parallel code study): at EP4 the ONLY blocker is
      # `moe_ep_size == 1` (modelopt_quant.py:2426; the other 14 clauses pass,
      # machine-checked). The kernel is ALREADY rank-offset-aware

      # DEFAULT OFF, PROVEN THREE WAYS, NOT ASSERTED: (1) the CPU test

      # PROVENANCE -- READ THIS BEFORE ANY REBASE ONTO origin/main. Upstream PR
      # #45298 (adubkov, "[Feat] moe: B12x W4A16 E4M3 K/32 route with EP support
      # for ModelOpt mixed checkpoints", head a3dde82553, STATE OPEN, 33 files

      # THE OTHER ~30 FILES ARE DELIBERATELY DROPPED, and a future merge that

      # group_size == 32), which our checkpoint can never satisfy (modelopt NVFP4

      # THE THREE DEFECTS #45298 HAS AGAINST OUR TREE -- each one is a SILENT
      # WRONG-RESULT bug or a hard TypeError, and each is re-imported by a merge.
      # (1) source_format="modelopt_e4m3_k32" DOES NOT EXIST in the installed
      #     flashinfer 0.7.2. _SOURCE_FORMATS
      #     (fused_moe/cute_dsl/blackwell_sm12x/moe_w4a16_prepare.py:23-30)
      #     accepts modelopt | modelopt_nvfp4 | fp4_e8m0_k32 | compressed_tensors
      #     and _normalize_source_format (:210-216) raises on anything else. The
      #     k32 spelling arrives with flashinfer #6824, still unreleased. Our
      #     export IS the K/16 E4M3 grid in swizzled storage -- per-expert
      #     weight_scale float8_e4m3fn [N, K/16], read from the safetensors
      #     headers: gate_proj (640,160) for K=2560, down_proj (2560,40) for
      #     K=640 -- which is exactly what prepare_w4a16_modelopt_nvfp4_weights
      #     documents (:711-716) and returns as scale_format="e4m3_k16" (:695), so
      #     B12X_W4A16_SOURCE_FORMAT = "modelopt". (fp4_e8m0_k32 would demand an
      #     E8M0 [E,N,ceil(K/32)] grid, _validate_e8m0_k32_scales:235-250;
      #     compressed_tensors INVERTS the global scale, _source_global_scale
      #     :226-233.) Pinned both ways by
      #     test_upstream_pr_spelling_is_rejected_by_this_wheel and
      #     test_source_format_is_a_real_wheel_key -- the first one is a test
      #     against THE UPSTREAM PR's OWN STRING, so it fails the day someone
      #     merges #45298 blind.
      # (2) THE ALPHA FOLD -- the worst of the three, 534x / 415x, and completely
      #     invisible in a throughput log. The PR passes layer.g1_alphas =
      #     w13_input_scale * w13_weight_scale_2 into a W4A16 kernel that IGNORES
      #     activation scales (moe_w4a16_prepare.py:711-716 "activation input
      #     scales are not folded into W4A16 weight preparation"; b12x_moe.py
      #     documents input_global_scale/fc2_input_scale as ignored for
      #     quant_mode=w4a16), and _process_nvfp4_packed_global_scale (:185-198)
      #     multiplies whatever we pass straight into the per-block scale fold at
      #     prep time -- so an activation scale inside that value is applied on
      #     top of the activation quantisation the kernel does not do. OUR
      #     checkpoint carries REAL calibrated input scales (measured from the
      #     tensors: gate/up 0.00187174, down_proj 0.00240653), so the product is
      #     wrong by 1/0.00187174 = 534x on gate/up and 1/0.00240653 = 415x on
      #     down. THE FIX IS NOT AT THE CALL SITE: b12x is folded into this file's
      #     EXISTING activation-neutralization site (modelopt_quant.py, next to
      #     the CuTe-DSL W4A16 precedent at :2885 and the marlin one), so
      #     g1_alphas/g2_alphas are STRUCTURALLY weight-only (= w13_weight_scale_2
      #     / w2_weight_scale_2, already EP-sliced by entry 205's _slice_scale)
      #     while every reader keeps seeing one "alpha" convention. A call-site
      #     patch would have left the folded product alive for the next consumer.
      #     Pinned by test_b12x_w4a16_alphas_are_weight_only (g1_alpha ==
      #     weight_scale_2 AND g1_alpha == product / input_scale) plus
      #     test_w4a16_never_folds_the_activation_scale and
      #     test_cutlass_route_never_prepares_and_keeps_the_folded_alpha. A
      #     related guard: a checkpoint whose gate and up GLOBAL scales differ is
      #     REFUSED (test_distinct_gate_up_global_scales_are_rejected), because
      #     one alpha cannot carry two.
      # (3) w13_layout IS NOT A CONSTANT. The PR hardcodes "w13", which is right
      #     FOR ITS OWN GATE -- that gate requires flashinfer_cutlass, where
      #     load_up_proj_weight_first is true and storage is [Up, Gate]. This
      #     route is a DIFFERENT backend, storage stays [Gate, Up], and the correct
      #     wheel value is "w31". Getting it wrong is not an error and not a
      #     slowdown: it silently swaps gate and up INSIDE SwiGLU. So the string is
      #     DERIVED FROM THE SAME PREDICATE THE LOADER USES --
      #     b12x_w4a16_w13_layout(up_first) with B12X_W4A16_W13_LAYOUT="w31" and
      #     B12X_W4A16_W13_LAYOUT_UP_FIRST="w13" -- and pinned in both directions
      #     (test_layout_strings_are_the_w31_w13_pair,
      #     test_layout_strings_mean_what_this_route_needs,
      #     test_up_proj_order_is_not_flipped_for_b12x).
      # THE OPT-IN CONTRACT (all of it enforced at ARG-RESOLUTION time, so a wrong
      # boot stops before the first forward instead of inside a kernel):
      #   * --moe-runner-backend flashinfer_b12x_w4a16 (named explicitly; `auto`
      #     never picks it), quantization modelopt_fp4 or modelopt_mixed;
      #   * --moe-a2a-backend none REQUIRED -- the route takes global expert ids
      #     plus its own expert map and has no dispatch/combine kernels. That is
      #     already production, and it is why this is a low-friction arm;
      #   * ep_size in {1, tp_size}, and expert data parallelism rejected outright
      #     (moe_dp_size not in (None, 0, 1) raises);
      #   * LoRA REJECTED -- the route repacks expert weights IN PLACE, so a
      #     merged/adapter weight would be repacked underneath it. The message
      #     sends you to flashinfer_cutlass. This is an arg-time refusal, NOT a
      #     tested live invalidation path (see UNTESTED);
      #   * --disable-shared-experts-fusion is set AUTOMATICALLY with a WARNING
      #     (overrides.py:_moe_runner_fusion_disable) -- the packed FP4 buffers
      #     cannot carry a BF16 shared expert and a fused expert breaks the
      #     ep_size * num_local == num_experts map arithmetic;
      #   * flashinfer MoE autotune is WARNED-ABOUT, not rejected: the CUTLASS
      #     autotune cache does not cover this route, so any tuned numbers you
      #     collected for flashinfer_cutlass are simply inert here;
      #   * CUDA_HOME=/usr/local/cuda-13.0 REQUIRED ON THIS BOX, for a reason worth
      #     remembering about flashinfer generally: the gate calls
      #     flashinfer.jit.cpp_ext.get_cuda_version(), which SHELLS OUT TO
      #     $CUDA_HOME/bin/nvcc (12.9 behind the default /usr/local/cuda symlink),
      #     NOT to the CUDA torch links (2.14.1+cu130). So a cu130 torch with a
      #     12.9 toolkit prefix still fails, and flashinfer_b12x_w4a16_unavailable_
      #     reason (flashinfer_cutlass.py:156-194) exists to say exactly that and
      #     name the fix. It also refuses any capability != (12,0) -- SM120 only,
      #     SM121 included NOT (the message says "this device is smXY").
      # THE REPIN IS THE EXPENSIVE PART, WHICH IS WHY THIS STAYS OFF UNTIL A GPU
      # WINDOW (9c.sh lines 496-521 carries the full measurement-free reasoning):
      # the flashinfer JIT cache DIRECTORY key (…/flashinfer/0.7.2/120f, 116
      # objects / 103 MiB today) does NOT contain the toolkit version, but
      # CUDA_HOME is baked into the ninja RULE (cached_ops/*/build.ninja line 3),
      # so repinning the variable changes every compile command and forces a
      # ONE-TIME full re-JIT -- fused_moe_120 alone is ~4-5 minutes. TRAP:
      # repointing the /usr/local/cuda symlink instead leaves every hash
      # IDENTICAL while swapping the compiler underneath (stale 12.9 objects under
      # a 13.0 nvcc). Set the variable; never move the symlink. It also changes
      # which libcudart sgl-kernel preloads, PROCESS-GLOBAL, so once adopted it
      # must be pinned in BOTH run scripts permanently or the re-JIT recurs on
      # every flip between arms. And any flashinfer autotune cache captured under
      # the old prefix should be treated as suspect.
      # BOOT NEEDLES -- AND ONE FABRICATION CAUGHT, which is exactly the failure
      # class entry 246 catalogued for the harness arms. The planned needle
      # "b12x W4A16 weights prepared" DOES NOT EXIST ANYWHERE IN THE TREE
      # (grep-verified over python/ and test/ at 4c91151ed0). The strings that
      # ACTUALLY print, each read from the source that emits it:
      #   "FlashInfer b12x W4A16 weight preparation took %.2f s for the first MoE
      #     layer (%d local experts, hidden %d, intermediate %d); the route is CUDA
      #     13 gated and repacks weights in place."  -- modelopt_quant.py, INFO,
      #     ONCE PER PROCESS by _B12X_W4A16_PREP_LOGGED. The preparer loops per
      #     expert in Python, so this line is the per-layer repack ESTIMATE for the
      #     other 47 layers; expect one per scheduler rank.
      #   "Precompiled b12x W4A16 MoE routes for Qwen4-Exp"  -- qwen4_exp.py:3567,
      #     INFO, once per rank, printed only if the prewarm warmed >=1 capacity.
      #   "b12x W4A16 prewarm: warmed %d token capacities (hidden %d, top_k %d, %d
      #     local experts, ep_size %d) on %s"  -- flashinfer_cutlass.py, INFO.
      #   "FlashInfer b12x W4A16 MoE is enabled. --disable-shared-experts-fusion is
      #     automatically set."  -- overrides.py, WARNING, at resolution.
      # A GREEN BOOT IS: the fusion WARNING, the preparation line, the warmed line,
      # and "Precompiled ..." x1 per rank, with ZERO "prewarm stopped" lines.
      # TWO FAILURE SIGNATURES, and note which side of the wheel each comes from:
      #   * "<something> is not initialized for CUDA graph capture; ..." --
      #     flashinfer's own text (experimental/b12x/moe/_shared/kernels/w4a16/
      #     route_pack.py:113, kernel.py:12570/:12874). It means THE PREWARM MISSED
      #     A SHAPE: the workspace cache refuses to allocate during a capture. In a
      #     server log it surfaces INSIDE our warning line "b12x W4A16 prewarm
      #     stopped at %d tokens (%s: %s)" (that is the ladder giving up at that
      #     capacity, so a graph captured above it dies at replay), and a
      #     "prewarm stopped" WARNING with no following "warmed" INFO means nothing
      #     at all was prewarmed and the boot is unsafe.
      #   * "requires CUDA 13 or later" -- matches BOTH our arg-time refusal
      #     ("requires CUDA 13 or later, but flashinfer's JIT gate sees CUDA
      #     {major}. … Fix: export CUDA_HOME …") and flashinfer's own
      #     ValueError at fused_moe/runners.py:7933 ("SM12x NVFP4 x BF16 requires
      #     CUDA 13 or later.", siblings :7481 FP8, :7724 MXFP8xMXFP4, :8141 SM12x
      #     NVFP4, :8300 b12x unified). If the startup dies naming the backend,
      #     $CUDA_HOME is missing or wrong, and the fix is the env, not the tree.
      # HOW THE GRAPH CONSTRAINT IS HANDLED (the design, because it will look
      # redundant later): the packed route table specializes on
      # route_pack_token_capacity(tokens) = the next power of two, and the W4A16
      # workspace raises if it must allocate inside a capture
      # (b12x moe_dispatch.py:2952-2958). So qwen4_exp.precompile_kernels_after_
      # loading drives the REAL fused func down a power-of-two ladder built by
      # b12x_w4a16_token_capacities() up to the largest captured decode/verify
      # batch -- descending, so the biggest allocation happens first and the cache
      # keeps the largest capacity per geometry key, one buffer covering every
      # tier. Prefill is NOT capture-constrained and its intermediates scale as
      # tokens * top_k * intermediate_size and are never released, so prefill
      # shapes cost hundreds of MB permanently and are behind
      # SGLANG_B12X_W4A16_PREWARM_PREFILL_TOKENS (0 = decode tiers only).
      # A PREWARM FAILURE NEVER FAILS THE BOOT -- deliberate, and it caught a real
      # bug during development: an earlier revision raised inside its own
      # try/except and would have silently warmed nothing.
      # EP PLUMBING (the part that decides whether this is correct at all):
      # expert_map is int32[num_experts] holding this rank's contiguous chunk and
      # -1 elsewhere. The PR's use of
      # flashinfer.experimental.b12x.moe.ep_moe.prepare_ep_expert_map is NOT
      # copied -- that belongs to the sibling b12x.* package launch_sm120_moe does
      # not consume -- but the divisibility and value-range checks it performs ARE
      # (test_indivisible_split_is_asserted). B12x is added to
      # _keeps_global_expert_ids because it consumes GLOBAL ids. The FP8 NEXTN
      # draft falls back to triton under the new pin exactly as it does under
      # flashinfer_cutlass (fp8.py), so 9c.sh checklist (l) -- assert the resolved
      # PAIR, target flashinfer_cutlass/triton pin, draft triton -- needs the same
      # reading here with target = flashinfer_b12x_w4a16. Entry 244's
      # _smallm_ineligibility_reason runner_config wiring survives untouched and
      # is unreachable for this backend (its enable_flashinfer_cutlass_moe input
      # is false), which test_predicates_do_not_leak_into_the_cutlass_route pins.
      # HONEST EXPECTATION -- write this down BEFORE the first A/B so nobody has
      # to discover it in a result table: THIS IS NOT A bs1 WIN. flashinfer's two
      # fastest small-M paths, direct_topk_routes (m <= 6) and tc_decode (m <= 4),
      # BOTH require expert_map is None, and EP4 ALWAYS passes a map. Our decode
      # traffic concentrates at bs p50 5-6, i.e. exactly the regime that drops to
      # packed-route execution. The credible wins are (a) ACCEPTANCE-LENGTH
      # RECOVERY -- BF16 activations, no FP4 activation quant, addressing the
      # measured bs1 128-256k regression 2.830 (FP8) -> 2.710 (NVFP4) that ate most
      # of the FP4 step-time win (entry 243), and (b) bs >= 8 (p90 ~16). Capacity
      # is UNCHANGED: same FP4 weights, and reuse_input_storage=True means the
      # repack does NOT take a second copy (asserted at prep time:
      # "FlashInfer W13 preparation did not reuse source storage"). Anyone quoting
      # a KV-token or GB-freed gain from this port is quoting entry 232/243 again.
      # VERIFICATION ON MAIN (first time this port coexists with today's
      # 244/245/246 commits). Prod venv, PER-FILE, CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=9,
      # PYTHONPATH=/root/sglang/sglang/python, at 4c91151ed0. 18 targets, ALL rc=0
      # and ALL EXACT vs the branch (log /tmp/opencode/integ-s-20261020/
      # battery.log): b12x route 67P/46sub; smallm 28P/66sub (entry 244's pin
      # intact -- the b12x route did NOT re-break the path its stub used to mask);
      # nvfp4_moe_sm120 3P/15S; kernels_namespace 76P (no KernelSpec added -- the
      # route reaches flashinfer, nothing of ours enters the registry);
      # deferred_scales 5P/4sub; modelopt_nvfp4 9P/5sub; standard_dispatcher 12P;
      # fp4_gemm_backend_sm120 12P; nvfp4_moe_backend_selection 3P;
      # modelopt_nvfp4_moe_dispatch 3P/5sub; spec dir 335P/9S/180sub; test_qsa
      # 0F/76P/140S; test_qsa_fused_kv_prepare 0F/12P/146S; the guardrail set a new
      # backend choice can trip -- test_server_args+docs 307P/128sub, whole
      # server_args/ dir 430P/225sub, resolution_hook_registry 10P, docs_coverage
      # 4P, exportable_env_vars 4P; check_registered_tests.py and
      # check_no_bare_pytest_main.py both rc=0; ruff
      # --select=F401,F821,F823,UP037 and ruff format --check clean on the 12 .py
      # files (the 13th is the .mdx); 7-module import smoke OK. Equivalence to the
      # tested branch: `git diff apply-s-20261020 HEAD` EMPTY, range-diff both
      # patches `=`.
      # UNTESTED -- THE GPU WINDOW OWES ALL OF THIS (nothing below has ever been
      # EXECUTED; 67 passing tests are CONTRACT tests that mock the wheel, so not
      # one number here is a measurement): every NUMERIC property (route output
      # vs the CUTLASS route, the w31 layout actually matching SwiGLU semantics on
      # hardware, the weight-only alpha actually being right end-to-end);
      # CAPTURE-SAFETY of the kernel at 188-SM Max-Q, including whether the
      # descending ladder really covers every tier NEXTN captures; the prewarm's
      # REAL boot cost and workspace BYTES (the ~3.7 MB/layer order-of-magnitude
      # reasoning for the sibling small-row kernel is NOT this route's number);
      # moe_tp_size == 1 AT TP4/EP4 -- INFERRED from moe_dp 1 plus the standard
      # dispatcher, never exercised; the int32 expert-map id equivalence under
      # real dispatch; reuse_input_storage buffer sharing at RUNTIME (only the
      # data_ptr assertions run on CPU); and weight-update / LoRA invalidation,
      # which is REFUSED AT ARG TIME and therefore has no live test that an
      # update_weights_from_disk call cannot corrupt a prepared layer.
      # FOLLOW-UPS: (1) a harness arm b12x_w4a16_on using the TRUE needles above
      # (and the two failure signatures as FORBIDs) -- do NOT write the
      # fabricated one; (2) the KL / GSM8K / accept-length A/B against the CUTLASS
      # FP4 route, which is a TWO-RESTART experiment by construction (the CUDA_HOME
      # repin is per-process and the first boot pays the re-JIT); (3) per-layer
      # repack timing, read off the once-per-process preparation line; (4) SEND
      # THE THREE DEFECTS UPSTREAM -- (1) and (3) are silent-wrong on any
      # gate-first or K/16 checkpoint, (2) is silent-wrong on any checkpoint with
      # calibrated input scales, which is every real ModelOpt export.
      # STATUS: on main, NOT pushed (detain is behind). LIVE ONLY AFTER RESTART
      # (user-driven), and even then INERT until someone names the backend AND
      # repins CUDA_HOME.

        248. 1818e2c0fb  FORK PATCH, no upstream PR behind it  ROPE-CACHE DEDUP -- the get_rope memo key carried the CALLER's raw max_position_embeddings, so the target and the NEXTN draft each built their OWN (1048576, 64) fp32 cos/sin cache: 256 MiB per rank of BIT-IDENTICAL duplication, 1 GiB over TP4, allocated before the KV pool is sized. The key is now built from what gets CONSTRUCTED, not from the argument the caller happened to pass
      # WHAT THE BUG COST, MEASURED. On the live geometry (nvidia/Qwen3.8-
      # Flash-Next-NVFP4, TP4, --context-length 1000000 with the YaRN override
      # factor=4.0 / original_max_position_embeddings=262144, head_dim 256 x
      # partial_rotary_factor 0.25 = rotary_dim 64, cache fp32 on CUDA):
      #   rows = original_max_position_embeddings * factor = 1048576; width 64;
      #   4 B  =>  1048576 * 64 * 4 = 268,435,456 B = 256 MiB per rope object.
      # The target and the draft each had one, and they were the same tensor
      # content -- torch.equal(a.cos_sin_cache, b.cos_sin_cache) is True -- so
      # this was pure duplication, NOT a numerical divergence and not a
      # correctness bug in the outputs. Two facts make the bytes material:
      # rope is built DURING model load, before the KV pool is sized, so the
      # pool was paying for it; and the FP4 arm's observed floor mid-run is
      # 0.59 GiB free, so 256 MiB/rank is not a rounding error here.
      # AFTER the first forward the saving is 128 MiB/rank, not 256, because
      # MRotaryEmbedding.forward_cuda calls _match_cos_sin_cache_dtype(query)
      # and re-casts the cache to bf16 in place. Quote 256 MiB/rank for the
      # allocation-time headroom and 128 MiB/rank for steady state; they are
      # the same fix at two different moments.
      # WHY THE TWO KEYS DIFFERED. configs/model_config.py:1182-1206
      # (_derive_context_length) rewrites hf_text_config.max_position_embeddings
      # to the user's --context-length, but ONLY for the draft model
      # (is_draft_model branch, the "Overriding the draft model's
      # max_position_embeddings to 1000000" warning). The target keeps the
      # checkpoint's 262144. get_rope's memo key included that raw argument,
      # so the two builds hashed differently and each got its own entry --
      # even though the yarn / yarn+mrope_section / deepseek_yarn branches
      # never hand the caller's max_position to their constructor at all:
      # they pass rope_scaling["original_max_position_embeddings"] positionally
      # and derive BOTH the cache length (torch.arange(original * factor)) and
      # the inv_freq correction range from that value alone. Two calls build
      # the same tensors iff (class, the positional the ctor receives, the
      # resolved length) agree -- which is exactly what the key now carries.
      # THE FALSE ALARM THAT LED HERE, recorded so nobody re-chases it: a
      # tokenizer warning about a 727,894-token prompt implied the context was
      # smaller than the traffic. It is not. YaRN factor 4.0 x
      # original_max_position_embeddings 262144 = 1,048,576 rows and the cache
      # really has them; sglang's own derived context says 262,144 because
      # utils/hf_transformers/common.py:682-698 (get_context_length) sets
      # rope_scaling_factor = 1 whenever "original_max_position_embeddings" is
      # in the scaling dict, so the factor never multiplies the derived
      # length. That file is UNTOUCHED by this entry, deliberately: the
      # derivation is what keeps --context-length validation honest, and the
      # warning is a reporting artifact, not a capacity limit.
      # THE FIX -- python/sglang/srt/layers/rotary_embedding/factory.py,
      # +240/-22, the ONLY source file changed:
      #   * _rope_cache_key (:276) is now TEN fields: the old eight MINUS
      #     max_position, PLUS _ambient_device_tag() (:125), the _rope_geometry
      #     tuple (:256) and a for_cpu discriminator.
      #   * _rope_geometry (:256, over _resolve_rope_geometry :155) mirrors the
      #     factory's dispatch BRANCH FOR BRANCH (:155) and returns
      #     (the class the factory will build, the max_position_embeddings the
      #     CONSTRUCTOR is handed, the resolved cache rows). Every branch whose
      #     class really does read the caller's value keeps it -- plain, mrope,
      #     llama3, linear, dynamic, dynamic-alpha, Bailing video_rope,
      #     longrope, proportional, dual-chunk, and any YaRN config that OMITS
      #     original_max_position_embeddings and so falls back to max_position.
      #     Those stay exactly as strict as before; only the branches that
      #     provably ignore the argument collapse together.
      #   * It is wrapped so it can NEVER raise: an unanalysable scaling type
      #     returns the conservative fallback ("", max_position, None), i.e. a
      #     key that shares nothing it is not sure about. A geometry helper
      #     that raised would fail a boot that the old key happily served.
      #   * _canonical_scaling_args (:141) now sorts its items. The old key
      #     was INSERTION-ORDER-SENSITIVE, so two configs carrying the same
      #     scalars in a different dict order already built two caches -- a
      #     latent second duplication this fix also closes.
      #   * _arange_rows (:120) = len(torch.arange(float)), i.e. CEIL, the real
      #     row formula for the scaling branches. Computing rows with int()
      #     would have keyed two different caches as the same entry, which is
      #     the dangerous direction.
      #   * get_rope_cpu (:678-690) now shares the builder with for_cpu=True.
      #     Its old 7-tuple was only ACCIDENTALLY disjoint from the CUDA key;
      #     that is no longer something to rely on.
      #   * WHAT WAS NOT DONE, on purpose, and it is the design decision here:
      #     the sub-fields of rope_parameters are NOT enumerated individually.
      #     A subset would make EVERY omitted knob a silent sharing bug (the
      #     exact class of defect entries 244 and 246 are about); class +
      #     ctor-positional + the full canonical dict is strictly stronger and
      #     needs no maintenance as knobs are added. Also untouched:
      #     model_config.py (the draft rewrite is load-bearing for non-YaRN
      #     derived lengths), common.py:682-698, and there is NO env var, NO
      #     default flip and NO change to cache invalidation/clearing.
      # THE RED/GREEN IS MACHINE-CHECKED, and the number in my own working
      # notes was wrong. Re-measured for this entry, PROD venv

      # BATTERY, all reproduced at CVD=9 and unchanged by the fix (the point

        249. no code  THE 2026-10-21 /root BATCH -- both run scripts take THREE identical-shape functional lines (L3 cap 50G->150G, metadata TTL 300->30, NEW --random-seed 20260930), with the seed's limits stated plainly and my own stale store-size premise corrected; the A/B harness gets PER-RUN HiCache STORE ISOLATION and the base-corruption repair plus a provenance guard that makes the class of failure impossible; and a POWER note that I had WRONG in this entry and corrected in place at §D: max_limit is 325 W on GPU2/GPU3 only (GPU0/GPU1 sit at 300 W, exactly as both run scripts say), so with TP4 lockstep the electrical headroom is 25 W on half the box and the EV is LOW
      # FILES OUTSIDE THE REPO (like entries 245/246/242/243): /root/run_-
      # sglang_qwenflashnext9c.sh (the FP4 arm), /root/run_sglang_qwenflash-
      # next9.sh (the FP8 arm), /root/ab_harness (not a git repo). NOTHING in

      # PROOF (re-verified for this entry, and the numbers in the working

      #   9.sh   c069ec66fed0ddf941c80003ae1e5617 -> 5db87f987f53e5159516b4599d64277f

      # trailing line, the tee). The offline prepare_server_args resolve is a
      # 539-field diff containing EXACTLY ONE changed field,
      # random_seed: None -> 20260930, which is the machine proof that nothing
      # else moved. NOTE the exec-line counts quoted as "60->61 / 50->51" are
      # NON-BLANK non-comment lines; a naive `grep -vc '^ *#'` includes blanks
      # and reports 63 / 54 on these files today. Use the md5, not a count.
      # 2. SEED HONESTY -- READ THIS BEFORE CITING A REPRODUCIBLE BOOT
      # --random-seed 20260930 is NOT a bit-identical-output pin and it is
      # NOT demonstrable by an A/B harness re-run. Three code facts make
      # greedy traffic RNG-free already: sampling_params.py:250-255 maps
      # temperature < 1e-10 to top_k = 1; sampler.py:1320-1323 returns the
      # logits VERBATIM for an all-greedy batch; eagle_utils.py:860-877
      # argmaxes when is_all_greedy. And /root/ab_harness benches at
      # temperature 0.0 -- SEED = "20260930" is defined at
      # lib/bench_profiles.py:41, EMITTED into every phase's argv at :392, and
      # enforced by the "missing --seed" guard at :545 (lib/bench_runner.sh:342
      # only RECORDS it, so grepping the runner for the literal misleads). So a
      # harness sweep shows the SAME NUMBERS with or without this pin, and that
      # is NOT evidence the pin failed.
      # WHAT IT DOES BUY: (i) reproducibility for temp>0 / top_p<1 traffic,
      # the only traffic where sampling draws coins at all; (ii) the BOOT-PATH
      # RNG consumers, which are the real confound -- model_runner.py:776,
      # Scheduler.__init__, the NEXTN/EAGLE3 draft's init_new, the DeepGEMM
      # warmup's torch.manual_seed, the Triton autotuner's RNG and
      # radix_cache.py:1685. Concretely: the 10-20 and 10-21 FP4 boots had
      # DIFFERENT seeds, which is one of the reasons entry 243's A/B could not
      # be scored cleanly. That is a comparability fix, not a perf fix.
      # IT IS NOT UPSTREAM'S PREFERENCE: upstream deliberately randomizes
      # (PR #43, 159cc741e4). It is independent of
      # --enable-deterministic-inference, which uses a per-request
      # sampling_seed instead.
      # THE KL CAVEAT, and it is the one that bites: a pinned-seed green KL
      # run is NOT an accuracy verdict on its own. Same seed + same
      # accumulation order can converge to the SAME wrong answer (false
      # convergence). Never let a pinned-seed green be the only evidence.
      # 3. L3 CAP -- MY "~740 GB STORE" PREMISE WAS STALE; A RAISE IS FREE
      # The reasoning I had written down was: the store is ~740 GB, so raising
      # the cap costs ~250 GB of blocking unlinks before the server is ready.
      # WRONG, and the correction changes the decision. Measured with
      # `du -sb /root/hicache_store`: 199,126,318,144 B = 185.47 GiB across
      # 139,790 files -- because the 10-21 17:42 boot's own 50G drain ALREADY
      # deleted ~1.15M files. The evictor's constructor only runs the drain
      # when `_total_bytes > max_size_bytes`
      # (mem_cache/storage/file/lru_file_evictor.py:117-118, the
      # "eviction enabled: cap=... existing=... B (N entries)" line at :124
      # reports what it found), so:
      #   * raising a cap is FREE at boot -- the new cap is above the existing
      #     total, nothing is unlinked, the store keeps warming;
      #   * only LOWERING below the current per-rank holding pays, and that is
      #     the blocking-unlink storm (boot-time, before ready). The 17:42
      #     boot is the one that paid it; that cost is SPENT.
      # 4 x 150 GB fits: other tenants take 690.84 GB of the 1.8T filesystem,
      # leaving 575.8 GB of slack ABOVE the 100 GB global MIN_FREE_SPACE floor
      # (even 4x200 leaves 461 GB; breaking the floor needs ~4x231 GB).
      # THE KNOB SCOPE, the thing to keep straight: MAX_SIZE is PER RANK (one
      # LRUFileEvictor per process, each scanning only its own
      # _<model>_<tp_rank>_<tp_size> suffix); MIN_FREE_SPACE is the only
      # GLOBAL knob. So "50G" always meant 4 x 50G, and 150G means 4 x 150G.
      # Steady state is the watermark, not the cap: 150 x 0.85 (EVICTION_RATIO)
      # = 127.5 GB/rank = 510 GB aggregate => roughly 9.1-11.8 h of warm
      # retention vs ~3.0-3.9 h at the old cap.
      # WHAT THE NEXT BOOT SHOULD PRINT, and what to check that it did: the
      # per-rank evictor logs ONE INFO line at the END of its constructor
      # (lru_file_evictor.py:121-125) -- "HiCacheFile eviction enabled:
      # cap=150000000000 B, watermark=0.85, min_free=100000000000 B,
      # existing=<N> B (<M> entries)" -- and it should show existing= ~= that
      # rank's ~50 GB holding, NOT 42,494,887,632 (that number is the 10-21
      # 00:11:29 snapshot, i.e. history). Grep that line for the CAP; it is
      # the only place the value is echoed. Two things it does NOT echo:
      # METADATA_TTL (never logged at all -> read /proc/<newpid>/environ) and
      # the individual unlink decisions (logger.debug at :390).
      # THE CLAMP THAT CAN SILENTLY EAT A CAP RAISE, checked for this entry so
      # nobody re-derives it: the constructor clamps max_size against the
      # filesystem before use -- safe_max = fs_total - min_free_bytes, and a
      # cap above it is reduced with the WARNING "HiCacheFile max_size exceeds
      # filesystem capacity; clamping to ..." (:107-114). On this box fs_total =
      # 1,966,603,517,952 B and min_free 100G, so safe_max ~= 1.87 TB PER RANK
      # and 150G is nowhere near it -- no clamp. It fires only for absurd caps,
      # which is the reassuring half; the other half is that the clamp is
      # PER-PROCESS and compares ONE rank's cap against the WHOLE filesystem,
      # so it can never see the 4x aggregate pressure. That is what
      # min_free_bytes is for: _enforce_free_space_locked runs at boot (:192)
      # and on every write, independent of the cap.
      # AGGREGATE PRESSURE, measured fresh for this entry: `df -B1 /` on the
      # /dev/md0 filesystem = 1,966,603,517,952 total, 872,542,162,944 used,
      # 994,087,866,368 available. 4 x 150G = 600 GB of cap fits inside that
      # with ~394 GB to spare, i.e. ~294 GB above the 100 GB floor. Judge
      # growth with df, not with the cap.
      # PER-RANK SEMANTICS, mechanically: _is_storage_owner is
      # `(not is_mla_model) or tp_rank == 0`, so for this non-MLA model ALL
      # FOUR ranks run their own evictor over their own
      # _<model>_<tp_rank>_<tp_size> suffix -- MAX_SIZE really is 4 x 150G, and
      # the "rank N (MLA): eviction handled by rank 0" INFO at :96-99 should
      # NOT appear on this stack (if it does, something became MLA-shaped).
      # TTL 300 -> 30, the reason: a stale metadata-cache POSITIVE makes set()
      # short-circuit "already exists" and return success WITHOUT WRITING
      # (hicache_storage.py:613-619, the "Key ... already exists. Skipped."
      # branch is logger.debug so a missing write leaves NO INFO trace), so an
      # external `rm` of a cache file leaves a permanently-lieing index for the
      # TTL window. contains() re-reads TTL per lookup (383-392), so 30 s heals
      # an rm in <=30 s instead of <=5 min. Cost: 10x the stat traffic.
      # Upstream default is 5.0 s (environ.py:825), so 30 is still conservative.
      # WATCH: an explicit metadata_ttl key in --hicache-storage-backend-extra-
      # config OVERRIDES this env var (hicache_storage.py:453/:457, and the
      # evictor's own _cfg prefers extra_config over env) -- an env edit then
      # silently does nothing.
      # 4. POWER -- CORRECTED, AND DEMOTED. The scripts were right; I was wrong.
      # An earlier revision of this entry asserted "power.max_limit = 325 W on
      # ALL FOUR cards" and called 9c.sh's and 9.sh's "GPU0/GPU1 are hard-capped
      # at 300 W" comments stale. Re-measured directly:
      #   nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,power.limit,power.default_limit,power.min_limit,power.max_limit --format=csv
      #     0  300 W  300 W  250 W  300 W   <-- already AT its ceiling, NO headroom
      #     1  300 W  300 W  250 W  300 W   <-- already AT its ceiling, NO headroom
      #     2  300 W  300 W  250 W  325 W   <-- 25 W of headroom
      #     3  300 W  300 W  250 W  325 W   <-- 25 W of headroom
      # BOTH SCRIPT COMMENTS ARE CORRECT AS WRITTEN (they were never edited).
      # The throttling itself is real -- clocks 2095-2287 of 3090 MHz (70-74%),
      # util 98-99%, 233-293 W against a 300 W enforced limit,
      # clocks_throttle_reasons.active = 0x4 = SW POWER CAP, no thermal
      # component, HBM at 95.5% -- but the lever is SMALL: only GPU2/GPU3 have
      # 25 W, and a TP4 decode step is LOCKSTEP, so it is bounded by the slowest
      # rank and the two unraisable cards stay the constraint. Raising them buys
      # at most whatever straggler asymmetry happens to favour GPU2/GPU3 in a
      # given step, and could add jitter. VERDICT: LOW EV, operator-only
      # (`nvidia-smi -pl 325 -i 2,3`, reversible with `-pl 300 -i 2,3`, no code,
      # no restart needed to prepare it) -- explicitly NOT "the highest-EV
      # experiment on this machine", which is what this file wrongly claimed. The
      # honest framing: memory is not the constraint, the electrical ceiling is,
      # and it cannot be lifted on half the GPUs.
      # 5. THE A/B HARNESS (/root/ab_harness, not in git)
      # (i) PER-RUN HiCache STORE ISOLATION. The hazard was SILENT, not loud:
      # run_pending_ab.sh's L3_STORE fed ONLY the accounting, and ab_sweep.sh
      # read ZERO of the driver's AB_DRIVER_* variables -- so an arm booted
      # with PROD's /root/hicache_store from BASE_ENV while the harness
      # measured prod's directory and reported those bytes as the ARM's L3
      # numbers. New lib/l3_store.sh (242 L): ISOLATION IS THE DEFAULT, each
      # run gets /root/ab_harness_l3/<UTC stamp>, deliberately OUTSIDE
      # RESULTS_ROOT because --rerun-arms `cp -a`s the arm dirs; per-RUN not
      # per-arm on purpose, so the HiCache arms (38/39/40) and the 00 anchors
      # still see one shared cache state; compose_env_kv FORCES the resolved
      # path into the child env, over both BASE_ENV and an arm's own ENV rule
      # (loudly). Sharing prod is opt-in and noisy: --share-prod-l3 /
      # --no-l3-isolate / AB_L3_ISOLATE=0 sets l3_cache_contamination_risk in
      # meta.json. AB_L3_STORE (an explicit operator path) always wins. A disk
      # guard REFUSES to create a harness store on a filesystem with < 150 GB
      # free. Resolution now happens in THREE places -- prepare_run_dir,
      # cmd_live_prepare, and the ab_sweep.sh boot (FATAL exit 1), which is
      # what closes the --resume / --rerun-arms hole. Nothing in the harness
      # ever deletes a store; end_of_run prints the exact command.
      #   One REAL bug this caught: l3_resolve scraped the run record with
      #   `sed -n 's/[[:space:]]*"store":.../'` and NO leading `.*`, so on a
      #   COMPACT one-line JSON everything before the key survived into the
      #   value (mode came out `{"store":"...`, store came out `{path`) --
      #   the pretty format parsed fine, so a jq -c record would have sent
      #   --resume to a nonsense path. Anchor `.*` on BOTH sides when scraping
      #   JSON with sed.
      # (ii) THE BASE-CORRUPTION REPAIR, and the guard that retires the class.
      #   Root cause was NOT the selftest sandbox-escape family (already
      #   closed): ab_sweep.sh:47 carried its OWN stale default
      #   AB_BASE_SCRIPT = the FP8 9.sh, and hard-coded BASEDIR="$ABH/base",
      #   so ~0.5 s after the driver cut base/ from 9c.sh, the engine
      #   re-extracted it from 9.sh. Neither file was wrong alone; only the
      #   PAIR disagreed -- which is why every new check is a CONSISTENCY
      #   check and never a frozen literal. Repaired through the sanctioned
      #   path only (a driver --dry-run refresh), and re-verified for this
      #   entry from the artifacts: base/base_provenance.txt says
      #   script=/root/run_sglang_qwenflashnext9c.sh, tokens=83, env=13,
      #   model=nvidia/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-NVFP4, cut=2026-10-21T23:30:41Z,

        250. 903910505e  PR #42482 (open, head 179a0ed4fb)  LRUFileEvictor.reserve refuses a key that already has a pending write -- a duplicate writer used to settle the FIRST writer's reservation and drift the cap
      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/42482. WHAT: reserve()
      # double-admitted -- a second reserve for a key with an outstanding
      # pending write returned True and OVERWROTE the pending entry, so the
      # duplicate's commit()/abort() settled the first writer's
      # reservation: cap overshoot (bytes charged/released against the
      # wrong outstanding write, max_size_bytes accounting drifting per
      # occurrence) and wrong eviction accounting. THE FIX (+5/-1,
      # storage/file/lru_file_evictor.py:192-195): under _lock,
      # suffixed_key already in _pending_writes -> return False. Refusal
      # is an EXISTING, documented outcome ("no-op miss" to the caller);
      # the docstring's refusal list gains "the key already has a pending
      # write". The second writer degrades instead of corrupting the
      # first's state.
      # WHY FOR US: the file backend is LIVE L3 on both arms (entry 249:
      # the running boot carries --enable-hierarchical-cache; the 150G
      # MAX_SIZE raise is restart-pending), and our 10-28 per-replica dir
      # split means same-replica TP ranks still share one store -- any
      # racing duplicate admit permanently moved the cap the eviction
      # loop reasons over. Entries 250/251/252 are the store-health trio
      # for that geometry.
      # DEVIATIONS: none -- clean cherry-pick of open head 179a0ed4fb;
      # rides with the +60-line CPU regression in
      # test/registered/unit/mem_cache/test_hicache_file_lru_unit.py.
      # BATTERY (numbers are at the FINAL tranche tip d598eff7da, the
      # file covers entries 250+251 together):
      # test_hicache_file_lru_unit.py 45P/2sub; family battery 147P/50sub;
      # test/registered/unit/mem_cache/ 2045P/3028S/0F/3969sub, CVD=9.

        251. dbae0166a1  PR #42488 (open, head 803e44812f)  a failed unlink is a bounded SKIP, not a STOP -- one undeletable oldest page used to pin the LRU head and refuse every later capacity write
      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/42488. WHAT:
      # _evict_oldest's OSError path re-pinned the victim at the LRU HEAD
      # (move_to_end(last=False)) and returned "stop" -- the capacity
      # eviction loop aborted and every later write needing eviction got
      # refused. A single undeletable page (open handle, permission, IO
      # error) was therefore permanent head-of-line blocking. THE FIX
      # (+8/-8): keep the bytes charged (_total_bytes NOT decremented --
      # they are still on disk), re-insert the entry at MRU instead of
      # the head, return "skipped" so the bounded should_continue loop
      # tries other victims. "stop" now means an empty index ONLY; the
      # docstrings ("skipped": in-flight write OR failed unlink / "stop":
      # empty index) were updated to match.
      # WHY FOR US: entry 249's harness store-isolation work exists
      # because arms silently booted against PROD's store; the symptom
      # class this closes ("capacity writes refused" with the store
      # visibly below cap) had a mechanical cause here -- 150G L3 makes
      # capacity-driven eviction the COMMON path, not the corner case.
      # DEVIATIONS: none -- clean cherry-pick of open head 803e44812f;
      # +60-line CPU regression rides in the same lru test file (entry
      # 250 carries the file-level battery numbers).

        252. 0b5a7df7c7  PR #44422 (open, head 0276104d40)  HiCacheFile storage-dir creation tolerates the cross-process race -- exist_ok=True closes a check-then-makedirs TOCTOU that could crash rank init
      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/44422. WHAT:
      # HiCacheFile.__init__ guarded os.makedirs(self.file_path) with
      # `if not os.path.exists(...)` -- two rank-0 workers (the predicate
      # tp_rank == 0 and attn_cp_rank == 0 admits more than one process
      # per directory) could pass the check together and the second
      # creator's makedirs raised FileExistsError AT INIT. THE FIX
      # (1 line, hicache_storage.py): os.makedirs(..., exist_ok=True) --
      # absorbs exactly the race while STILL propagating genuine errors
      # (a permission-denied path is not "already exists", it raises and
      # the boot dies loudly, as it should).
      # WHY FOR US: our 10-28 per-replica dir split narrowed the sharing
      # but same-replica TP ranks still create the SAME directory, so
      # this crash class is live on our geometry, not hypothetical.
      # RED-PROOF, MACHINE-CHECKED (worker, at the landed diff):
      # reverting the one line reproduces the FileExistsError in the new
      # race regression -- the test is not vacuous.
      # DEVIATIONS: none -- clean cherry-pick of open head 0276104d40.
      # BATTERY: new test/registered/unit/mem_cache/
      # test_hicache_file_directory_race.py 4P (CVD=9); the whole-
