        254. d598eff7da  PR #44776 (open, head 5e26a3473e, ported)  optional parallel page READS in the file backend -- SGLANG_HICACHE_FILE_BACKEND_READ_WORKERS, DEFAULT OFF (EnvInt(1) = serial), order-preserving, raises-become-misses, and unioned with our entry-24 page-degrade catch

      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/44776. WHAT
      # (hicache_storage.py ~+77/-14 + environ.py +2 + docs row +5): with
      # read_workers > 1, _map_reads runs the FILE READS of one
      # batch_get/batch_get_v2 call on a lazily built thread pool
      # ("hicache-file-read") and returns only after EVERY read finished,
      # in INPUT ORDER -- so callers, prefetch acks and the cross-rank
      # reduce see results identical to the serial loop. A read that
      # raises becomes a miss (None -> False), never a failed batch.
      # reads_workers <= 1 or len(items) <= 1 short-circuits to the old
      # list-comprehension path -- DEFAULT OFF IS BYTE-IDENTICAL.
      # ARCHITECTURE PIN, not incidental: only the file read (into a
      # private staging page) runs on the pool; every host-pool write
      # (set_from_flat_data_page) STAYS ON THE CALLING THREAD, in page
      # order, because host pools may hold thread-local state there (the
      # unified host pool's layout lease -- calling it from a pool thread
      # can deadlock). Staging is chunked at 4 x read_workers so pool
      # reads never hold one page-buffer per queued item.
      # UNION WITH OUR ENTRY-24 PAGE-DEGRADE WORK -- THE DEVIATION FROM
      # THE PR HEAD, and the reason this entry is marked ported: the PR
      # left the calling-thread pool-write in _read_pages_parallel RAW
      # (an incompatible-layout page would raise out of the daemon
      # storage threads -- the exact failure class entry 24 made
      # per-page-miss). Our port wraps that half in the SAME
      # (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) catch + _report_bad_page the
      # serial _read_page uses, so a bad-layout page is a per-page miss
      # on the parallel path too (verified inline, workers=1 and 8); and
      # _batch_io_v2's serial fallback KEEPS our _safe_page_op wrapper
      # the PR had removed. Semantics of the PR preserved: same order,
      # same miss-on-raise, same default.
      # ENTRY-199 WRITE SEMANTICS UNCHANGED, stated plainly because the
      # wanted-file flagged a conflict: batched write-through lives in
      # the tree/scheduler layers and in the file backend's WRITE path --
      # this entry touches READS only; writes stay serial. No overlap in
      # the landed diff.
      # GPU-WINDOW DEBT (both OWED before raising workers on prod):
      # (i) parallel-read end-to-end against the REAL unified host pool
      # -- the CPU test stands in a flat-page pool, not the
      # layout-lease holder; (ii) re-test the upstream DEADLOCK CLAIM
      # the docstring relies on (pool-write-from-pool-thread), so the
      # calling-thread pin is re-verified on our pools, not taken on
      # faith.
      # BATTERY: new test/registered/hicache/
      # test_hicache_file_parallel_reads.py 5P CPU-only
      # (register_cpu_ci, base-a-test-cpu); tranche-wide numbers in the
      # header note.

    THE GENERALIZATION WORTH KEEPING: a memo key that includes a CALLER-CONTEXT
      argument (a length, a device, a mode) is a silent per-caller cache
      multiplication, and the duplicated objects are usually bit-identical -- so
      nothing ever looks wrong, you just pay twice. When two model copies (target
      + draft) are built from one process-global builder, check what the KEY
      contains versus what the CONSTRUCTOR receives. And fix the KEY, not the
      upstream value: the rewrite that made them differ was load-bearing.

      TESTS, and prefer a real-class fixture over a SimpleNamespace that can
      carry dead attributes. That single habit is the difference between a
      latent crash and a green CPU lane.

    the venv bump is the operator's call, so our pin + engine assert
    stay at 0.7.1 (requirements/pyproject + engine.py:1925 verified).
      [10-19 LATER THE SAME DAY: REVERSED by the operator's decision --
      2b46270c5d takes 44be30d216/#44591 and the prod venv moved to
      0.7.2. Full record in entry 228. Keep the reasoning as history but
      note that it was direction-correct and mechanism-wrong: direction-
      correct because the pin and the wheel must never be left split,
      mechanism-wrong because assert_pkg_version is a FLOOR, not an
      equality check (utils/common.py:2380, the parameter is literally
      named min_version) -- so new wheel + old pin BOOTS, and the box in
      fact ran that way for hours, while the pin going UP past an old
      wheel is what hard-fails boot. The reason the pin nevertheless had
      to land is quieter: the operator's rebuild command
      `uv pip install -e 'python[dev]'` re-resolves the `==0.7.1` pin and
      would have SILENTLY DOWNGRADED flashinfer back to 0.7.1 (run from
      /root/sglang/sglang, uv walks UP to the prod /root/sglang/.venv --
      there is no .venv in the repo root). Deferring the code pin until
      the wheel actually moved was sound; the stated failure mode was
      not the real one.]

  * BEHAVIOR WATCH -- NEXT RESTART (entries 248-249): FOUR THINGS, ONE OF WHICH
    IS A COUPLING THAT DID NOT EXIST BEFORE.
      - TARGET AND DRAFT NOW SHARE ONE ROPE CACHE (entry 248). They got the same
        ANSWERS before (the tensors were bit-identical), but they now share the
        SAME OBJECT, so anything that mutates it mutates both: the in-place bf16
        re-cast in _match_cos_sin_cache_dtype and any growth in
        _ensure_cos_sin_cache_length. Benign on today's config (both models are
        bf16; a cache too SHORT for either still gets its own entry because rows
        are in the key). The watch is for the day a per-model dtype, a different
        rotary_dim, or a per-model cache length is introduced -- that change is
        now a change to a shared object, and it will not announce itself as a
        rope change. Symptom to look for: one model's rope table behaving like the
        other's. Rollback is one commit (1818e2c0fb), revert-alone.
      - THE SEED PIN DOES NOTHING FOR GREEDY TRAFFIC (entry 249). If a harness
        re-run produces byte-identical numbers with and without --random-seed,
        that is EXPECTED: greedy bypasses the RNG and the harness benches at
        temperature 0.0. Judge the pin on temp>0/top_p<1 traffic and on the BOOT
        PATH (draft init_new, DeepGEMM warmup, autotuner RNG), whose whole purpose
        is that two boots become comparable. And never accept a pinned-seed green
        KL as an accuracy verdict by itself.
      - THE L3 STORE WILL GROW, ON PURPOSE, TO ~510 GB AGGREGATE (entry 249). Cap
        is now 150G PER RANK with a 127.5 GB/rank watermark, up from ~3.0-3.9 h of
        warm retention to ~9.1-11.8 h. Judge it with `df -B1 /root/hicache_store`
        and `/proc/<pid>/environ`. What the log does and does not tell you: each
        rank prints ONE INFO line at boot, "HiCacheFile eviction enabled:
        cap=150000000000 B, watermark=..., min_free=..., existing=... B (N
        entries)" (lru_file_evictor.py:121-125) -- grep THAT to confirm the cap
        landed. METADATA_TTL is never logged at all, and per-file unlink
        decisions are logger.debug, so "I see no eviction lines" proves nothing;
        absence of INFO eviction chatter is the normal state. The 100 GB GLOBAL
        MIN_FREE_SPACE is the real ceiling and the only knob that can make
        writes start refusing; watch it as the aggregate approaches ~1.7 TB used.
        A cap RAISE is free at boot; a LOWERING below a rank's holding is the
        blocking-unlink storm -- do not lower it casually.
      - POWER HEADROOM IS ON HALF THE BOX ONLY (entry 249 §D, corrected).
        max_limit is 325 W on GPU2/GPU3 and 300 W on GPU0/GPU1, and all four sit
        at 300 W today -- so the two cards that are throttling hardest CANNOT be
        raised. The box is genuinely SW-power-capped at 70-74% of clocks at 98%
        util with no thermal component, but a TP4 step is lockstep and bounded by
        the slowest rank, so `-pl 325 -i 2,3` is a LOW-EV operator experiment,
        not a lever. Both run scripts' existing comments on this are correct.
      - STANDING RESTART CAVEAT. The running FP4 boot (pid 4058818, 10-21
        17:42:20) already carries HiCache ON, --hicache-mamba-size-gb auto,
        --mem-fraction-static 0.88 and --max-mamba-cache-size 384 -- so the
        "HiCache/L3 is PARKED" statements in entries 245/246 are HISTORY, and
        max_total_num_tokens=8,012,096 CONFIRMS the sizing model. What is still
        restart-pending after this batch, and nothing else: SGLANG_HICACHE_FILE_-
        BACKEND_MAX_SIZE (live 50G), SGLANG_HICACHE_FILE_BACKEND_METADATA_TTL
        (live 300), --random-seed (absent from the live argv), and entry 248's
        rope dedup. All four land on the next restart the USER drives; until then
        a boot-level claim about them is unverifiable, and re-reading the current
        log cannot show any of them.

    ceiling = tp_size x MAX_SIZE -- divide by tp_size. Unset env => inert
    evictor + /tmp/hicache fallback (filled 980 GB once). MIN_FREE_SPACE is a
    floor, not a budget. --hicache-size and --hicache-ratio are mutually
    exclusive (absolute wins when > 0). #39880's fast_file backend inherits
    the same registry.

    silently downgraded flashinfer back to 0.7.1 (that command, run from
    /root/sglang/sglang, resolves UP to the prod venv -- there is no
    .venv in the repo root). No flashinfer-jit-cache / flashinfer-cubin
    package exists in this venv, so nothing had to be realigned. .venv2
    stays 0.6.18 and is still where CVD=9 batteries run; every FI-related
    number matched on BOTH wheels. Rollback ORDER matters: revert
    2b46270c5d FIRST (else the 0.7.2 floor assert kills boot), then run
    /tmp/opencode/fi-bump-072/ROLLBACK.sh -- 0.7.1 IS in the uv cache, so
    that step is offline-capable, unlike the 0.7.0.post1 rollback above.
